### 🔍 DUCKDB SEARCH STRATEGY

Use `execute_sql_query(sql)`. The `"samples"` field is your vocabulary.

⚠️ **CRITICAL SYNTAX RULES — violations cause runtime errors:**
1. Column names in double quotes MUST include outer brackets: `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` NOT `"Employee Master[Employee Name]"`. The `[...]` is part of the name.
2. UNNEST MUST use `AS t(rec)` alias pattern — then access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER use `AS pe` or `pe['field']` — DuckDB requires the `(rec)` destructuring.
3. Copy column/field names from the listings below **character-for-character** — do NOT abbreviate or reformat them.

**THREE COLUMN TYPES — READ CAREFULLY:**
- **Flat columns**: top-level columns, accessed as `"column_name"` directly.
- **Identifier columns**: unique-value columns (IDs, keys), use for JOINs and exact lookups.
- **Struct columns**: nested columns containing multiple fields, accessed via UNNEST.

**Rule 3 — struct columns**: Copy column/field names from the STRUCT block above EXACTLY.
- Flat columns are accessed as `"column_name"` — they are NOT inside any UNNEST.
- Struct fields are accessed as `rec['field_name']` — ONLY after UNNEST.
- NEVER use `t.rec["flat_col"]` for flat columns — use `"flat_col"` directly.
- ⚠️ UNNEST alias MUST be `AS t(rec)` — the `(rec)` is required. Access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER write `AS pe` or `pe['field']`.
```sql
-- ✅ CORRECT: UNNEST with AS t(rec), access fields as rec['field']
SELECT "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS rating
FROM enterprise_data
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL;

-- ❌ WRONG: Missing outer brackets on column name, wrong UNNEST alias
SELECT "Employee Master[Employee Name]", pe['Calculated Rating']  -- BOTH WRONG!
FROM enterprise_data CROSS JOIN UNNEST("...") AS pe;  -- pe['field'] does NOT work!

-- MULTIPLE structs: use SEPARATE aliases t1, t2 (NOT t.something)
SELECT "flat_col", t1.rec['field_a'] AS a, t2.rec['field_b'] AS b
FROM my_table
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_1") AS t1(rec)
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_2") AS t2(rec)
WHERE t1.rec['field_a'] = 'value';

-- Filter rows via list_filter (for boolean checks without UNNEST):
SELECT * FROM my_table
WHERE len(list_filter("struct_col", x -> x['field'] ILIKE '%term%')) > 0;

-- Aggregate inside structs:
SELECT "flat_col", SUM(rec['numeric_field']) AS total
FROM my_table CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col") AS t(rec)
GROUP BY "flat_col";
```
Inside structs, apply Rule 1 (freetext→ILIKE) or Rule 2 (categorical→=) based on each field's semantic_type.

**Rule 4 — search toolkit**:
- Case-insensitive: `ILIKE '%term%'` (default for user text search)
- Accent-insensitive: `lower(strip_accents("col")) ILIKE lower(strip_accents('%term%'))`
- Fuzzy names: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') >= 0.85`
- Multi-value OR: `regexp_matches("col", '(?i)(term1|term2)')`
- No vocabulary available: default to `ILIKE '%term%'`

The following functions have already been provided. Please use them as needed and do not redefine them.
<function>
def execute_sql_query(sql_query: str) -> pd.DataFrame
    """Execute a DuckDB SQL query and return the result as a pandas DataFrame."""
</function>


### 🔍 DUCKDB SEARCH STRATEGY

Use `execute_sql_query(sql)`. The `"samples"` field is your vocabulary.

⚠️ **CRITICAL SYNTAX RULES — violations cause runtime errors:**
1. Column names in double quotes MUST include outer brackets: `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` NOT `"Employee Master[Employee Name]"`. The `[...]` is part of the name.
2. UNNEST MUST use `AS t(rec)` alias pattern — then access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER use `AS pe` or `pe['field']` — DuckDB requires the `(rec)` destructuring.
3. Copy column/field names from the listings below **character-for-character** — do NOT abbreviate or reformat them.

**THREE COLUMN TYPES — READ CAREFULLY:**
- **Flat columns**: top-level columns, accessed as `"column_name"` directly.
- **Identifier columns**: unique-value columns (IDs, keys), use for JOINs and exact lookups.
- **Struct columns**: nested columns containing multiple fields, accessed via UNNEST.

**FLAT COLUMNS** (use `"col_name"` directly in SELECT/WHERE):
  → `"[CV Employee Summary[CV Employee Summary]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Age]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Assignment Status]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Basic Salary]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Child Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Cost of Living Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Date of Joining]]"` (datetime)
  → `"[Employee Master[Degree]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Department]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Division]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Educational Institute]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name (Arabic)]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Family Book Number]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Gender]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Grade]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Graduation Date]]"` (datetime)
  → `"[Employee Master[Housing Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Job Title]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Date]]"` (datetime)
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Reason]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Major (Education)]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Marital Status]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Nationality]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Children]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Spouses]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Office Name]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Organization Unit]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Person Type]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Phone Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Position Title]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Sector]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Sick Leave Taken]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Social Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Supervisor Name]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Supplementary Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Technical Special Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Time Since Last Promotion]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Total Entitlement Amount]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Annual Leave Entitlement]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Approved Annual Leave]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Approved Non-Mandatory Leave]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Approved Wellbeing Leave]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Non-Mandatory Leave Entitlement]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Wellbeing Leave Entitlement]]"` (float)

**IDENTIFIER COLUMNS** (use for JOINs and exact lookups only — do NOT filter by arbitrary values):
  → `"[Employee Master[Email Address]]"` (id_like, e.g. akalghfeli@ECOUNCIL.AE, eraldhaheri@ECOUNCIL.AE, nmsalmenhali@ECOUNCIL.AE)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Number]]"` (id_like, e.g. 1269, 1314, 1316)
  → `"[Employee Master[Passport Number]]"` (id_like, e.g. AA0095550, AA046978, AA0623273)

**STRUCT COLUMNS** (require UNNEST — see Rule 3):

**STRUCT: `"[CV Employee Competencies[Technical Competency Name]]"`** — fields:
- `rec['CV Employee Competencies[Technical Competency Name]']` (string, freetext)

- `rec['Employee Assignment History[Assignment Name]']` (string, categorical)
- `rec['Employee Assignment History[Position Title]']` (string, categorical)
- `rec['Employee Assignment History[Employee Grade]']` (string, categorical)
- `rec['Employee Assignment History[Assignment Start Date]']` (datetime)
- `rec['Employee Assignment History[Assignment End Date]']` (datetime)

**STRUCT: `"[Employee Previous Employer[Previous Employer Name][Previous Job Title][Start Date][End Date]]"`** — fields:
- `rec['Employee Previous Employer[Previous Employer Name]']` (string, categorical)
- `rec['Employee Previous Employer[Previous Job Title]']` (string, categorical)
- `rec['Employee Previous Employer[Start Date]']` (datetime)
- `rec['Employee Previous Employer[End Date]']` (datetime)

**STRUCT: `"[CV Employee Work Experience[CV Company Name][CV Job Title][CV Responsibilities Summary][CV Start Date][CV End Date]]"`** — fields:
- `rec['CV Employee Work Experience[CV Company Name]']` (string, freetext)
- `rec['CV Employee Work Experience[CV Job Title]']` (string, freetext)
- `rec['CV Employee Work Experience[CV Responsibilities Summary]']` (string, freetext)
- `rec['CV Employee Work Experience[CV Start Date]']` (datetime)
- `rec['CV Employee Work Experience[CV End Date]']` (datetime)

**Rule 1 — freetext columns**: Use `ILIKE '%word%'`. Find closest word in samples first. For typos: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') > 0.85`.
  → `"[CV Employee Summary[CV Employee Summary]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Educational Institute]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name (Arabic)]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Major (Education)]]"` is freetext

**Rule 2 — categorical columns**: Use `=` or `IN (...)` with exact values from samples.
  → `"[Employee Master[Assignment Status]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Degree]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Department]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Division]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Gender]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Grade]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Job Title]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Reason]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Marital Status]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Nationality]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Children]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Office Name]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Organization Unit]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Person Type]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Position Title]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Sector]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Supervisor Name]]"` is categorical

**Rule 3 — struct columns**: Copy column/field names from the STRUCT block above EXACTLY.
- Flat columns are accessed as `"column_name"` — they are NOT inside any UNNEST.
- Struct fields are accessed as `rec['field_name']` — ONLY after UNNEST.
- NEVER use `t.rec["flat_col"]` for flat columns — use `"flat_col"` directly.
- ⚠️ UNNEST alias MUST be `AS t(rec)` — the `(rec)` is required. Access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER write `AS pe` or `pe['field']`.
```sql
-- ✅ CORRECT: UNNEST with AS t(rec), access fields as rec['field']
SELECT "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS rating
FROM enterprise_data
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL;

-- ❌ WRONG: Missing outer brackets on column name, wrong UNNEST alias
SELECT "Employee Master[Employee Name]", pe['Calculated Rating']  -- BOTH WRONG!
FROM enterprise_data CROSS JOIN UNNEST("...") AS pe;  -- pe['field'] does NOT work!

-- MULTIPLE structs: use SEPARATE aliases t1, t2 (NOT t.something)
SELECT "flat_col", t1.rec['field_a'] AS a, t2.rec['field_b'] AS b
FROM my_table
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_1") AS t1(rec)
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_2") AS t2(rec)
WHERE t1.rec['field_a'] = 'value';

-- Filter rows via list_filter (for boolean checks without UNNEST):
SELECT * FROM my_table
WHERE len(list_filter("struct_col", x -> x['field'] ILIKE '%term%')) > 0;

-- Aggregate inside structs:
SELECT "flat_col", SUM(rec['numeric_field']) AS total
FROM my_table CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col") AS t(rec)
GROUP BY "flat_col";
```
Inside structs, apply Rule 1 (freetext→ILIKE) or Rule 2 (categorical→=) based on each field's semantic_type.

**Rule 4 — search toolkit**:
- Case-insensitive: `ILIKE '%term%'` (default for user text search)
- Accent-insensitive: `lower(strip_accents("col")) ILIKE lower(strip_accents('%term%'))`
- Fuzzy names: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') >= 0.85`
- Multi-value OR: `regexp_matches("col", '(?i)(term1|term2)')`
- No vocabulary available: default to `ILIKE '%term%'`

2026-05-07 14:59:08 [INFO] Code Generated:
# TODO: import the required dependencies
import pandas as pd

# Write code here
# To find the best employee, I need to look at performance ratings and determine who has the highest scores
# Based on the data schema, I can use the Employee Performance struct to find the highest calculated ratings

sql_query = """
SELECT 
    "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
    "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
    "[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
    "[Employee Master[Department]]",
    rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS calculated_rating,
    rec['Employee Performance[Normalized Performance Rating]'] AS normalized_rating,
    rec['Employee Performance[Performance Review Period]'] AS review_period
FROM enterprise_data
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
    AND rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] != ''
ORDER BY 
    CAST(rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS FLOAT) DESC
LIMIT 5;
"""

result_df = execute_sql_query(sql_query)

# Also check for supervisor ratings in Employee Competencies Rating for additional context
sql_query_2 = """
SELECT 
    "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
    "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
    "[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
    rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Competancy Name]'] AS competency,
    rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Employee Rating]'] AS employee_rating,
    rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] AS supervisor_rating
FROM enterprise_data
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Competencies Rating[Competancy Name][Employee Rating][Supervisor Rating][Employee Rating Description][Supervisor Rating Description]]") AS t(rec)
WHERE rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
    AND rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] != ''
ORDER BY 
    CAST(rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] AS FLOAT) DESC
LIMIT 5;
"""

result_df_2 = execute_sql_query(sql_query_2)

# Find the employee with the highest overall performance (both calculated rating and supervisor rating)
# For this analysis, I'll consider employees with high calculated ratings AND high supervisor ratings

sql_query_3 = """
WITH performance_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
        "[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
        "[Employee Master[Department]]",
        "[Employee Master[Email Address]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_calculated_rating,
        MAX(rec['Employee Performance[Normalized Performance Rating]']) AS best_rating_description
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(perf)
    WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]", "[Employee Master[Position Title]]", "[Employee Master[Department]]", "[Employee Master[Email Address]]"
),
competency_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_supervisor_rating
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Competencies Rating[Competancy Name][Employee Rating][Supervisor Rating][Employee Rating Description][Supervisor Rating Description]]") AS t(comp)
    WHERE rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
)
SELECT 
    p.[Employee Master[Employee Name]],
    p.[Employee Master[Employee Grade]],
    p.[Employee Master[Position Title]],
    p.[Employee Master[Department]],
    p.max_calculated_rating,
    p.best_rating_description,
    COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS max_supervisor_rating,
    COALESCE(p.max_calculated_rating, 0) + COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS combined_score
FROM performance_data p
LEFT JOIN competency_data c ON p."Employee Master[Employee Name]" = c."Employee Master[Employee Name]"
ORDER BY combined_score DESC
LIMIT 5;
"""

result_df_3 = execute_sql_query(sql_query_3)

# Declare result var: 
result = {
    "top_performer": result_df_3.iloc[0]["Employee Master[Employee Name]"] if len(result_df_3) > 0 else "No data available",
    "top_performer_details": result_df_3.iloc[0].to_dict() if len(result_df_3) > 0 else {},
    "top_performers_5": result_df_3.to_dict('records') if len(result_df_3) > 0 else []
}

print("Analysis Complete. Showing top performers based on calculated performance ratings and supervisor ratings.")
print(result)

The following functions have already been provided. Please use them as needed and do not redefine them.
<function>
def execute_sql_query(sql_query: str) -> pd.DataFrame
    """Execute a DuckDB SQL query and return the result as a pandas DataFrame."""
</function>


### 🔍 DUCKDB SEARCH STRATEGY

Use `execute_sql_query(sql)`. The `"samples"` field is your vocabulary.

⚠️ **CRITICAL SYNTAX RULES — violations cause runtime errors:**
1. Column names in double quotes MUST include outer brackets: `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` NOT `"Employee Master[Employee Name]"`. The `[...]` is part of the name.
2. UNNEST MUST use `AS t(rec)` alias pattern — then access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER use `AS pe` or `pe['field']` — DuckDB requires the `(rec)` destructuring.
3. Copy column/field names from the listings below **character-for-character** — do NOT abbreviate or reformat them.

**THREE COLUMN TYPES — READ CAREFULLY:**
- **Flat columns**: top-level columns, accessed as `"column_name"` directly.
- **Identifier columns**: unique-value columns (IDs, keys), use for JOINs and exact lookups.
- **Struct columns**: nested columns containing multiple fields, accessed via UNNEST.

**FLAT COLUMNS** (use `"col_name"` directly in SELECT/WHERE):
  → `"[CV Employee Summary[CV Employee Summary]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Age]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Assignment Status]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Basic Salary]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Child Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Cost of Living Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Date of Joining]]"` (datetime)
  → `"[Employee Master[Degree]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Department]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Division]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Educational Institute]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name (Arabic)]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Family Book Number]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Gender]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Grade]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Graduation Date]]"` (datetime)
  → `"[Employee Master[Housing Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Job Title]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Date]]"` (datetime)
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Reason]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Major (Education)]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Marital Status]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Nationality]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Children]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Spouses]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Office Name]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Organization Unit]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Person Type]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Phone Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Position Title]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Sector]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Sick Leave Taken]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Social Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Supervisor Name]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Supplementary Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Technical Special Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Time Since Last Promotion]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Total Entitlement Amount]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Annual Leave Entitlement]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Approved Annual Leave]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Approved Non-Mandatory Leave]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Approved Wellbeing Leave]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Non-Mandatory Leave Entitlement]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Wellbeing Leave Entitlement]]"` (float)

**IDENTIFIER COLUMNS** (use for JOINs and exact lookups only — do NOT filter by arbitrary values):
  → `"[Employee Master[Email Address]]"` (id_like, e.g. akalghfeli@ECOUNCIL.AE, eraldhaheri@ECOUNCIL.AE, nmsalmenhali@ECOUNCIL.AE)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Number]]"` (id_like, e.g. 1269, 1314, 1316)
  → `"[Employee Master[Passport Number]]"` (id_like, e.g. AA0095550, AA046978, AA0623273)

**STRUCT COLUMNS** (require UNNEST — see Rule 3):

**STRUCT: `"[CV Employee Competencies[Technical Competency Name]]"`** — fields:
- `rec['CV Employee Competencies[Technical Competency Name]']` (string, freetext)

**Rule 3 — struct columns**: Copy column/field names from the STRUCT block above EXACTLY.
- Flat columns are accessed as `"column_name"` — they are NOT inside any UNNEST.
- Struct fields are accessed as `rec['field_name']` — ONLY after UNNEST.
- NEVER use `t.rec["flat_col"]` for flat columns — use `"flat_col"` directly.
- ⚠️ UNNEST alias MUST be `AS t(rec)` — the `(rec)` is required. Access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER write `AS pe` or `pe['field']`.
```sql
-- ✅ CORRECT: UNNEST with AS t(rec), access fields as rec['field']
SELECT "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS rating
FROM enterprise_data
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL;

-- ❌ WRONG: Missing outer brackets on column name, wrong UNNEST alias
SELECT "Employee Master[Employee Name]", pe['Calculated Rating']  -- BOTH WRONG!
FROM enterprise_data CROSS JOIN UNNEST("...") AS pe;  -- pe['field'] does NOT work!

-- MULTIPLE structs: use SEPARATE aliases t1, t2 (NOT t.something)
SELECT "flat_col", t1.rec['field_a'] AS a, t2.rec['field_b'] AS b
FROM my_table
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_1") AS t1(rec)
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_2") AS t2(rec)
WHERE t1.rec['field_a'] = 'value';

-- Filter rows via list_filter (for boolean checks without UNNEST):
SELECT * FROM my_table
WHERE len(list_filter("struct_col", x -> x['field'] ILIKE '%term%')) > 0;

-- Aggregate inside structs:
SELECT "flat_col", SUM(rec['numeric_field']) AS total
FROM my_table CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col") AS t(rec)
GROUP BY "flat_col";
```
Inside structs, apply Rule 1 (freetext→ILIKE) or Rule 2 (categorical→=) based on each field's semantic_type.

**Rule 4 — search toolkit**:
- Case-insensitive: `ILIKE '%term%'` (default for user text search)
- Accent-insensitive: `lower(strip_accents("col")) ILIKE lower(strip_accents('%term%'))`
- Fuzzy names: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') >= 0.85`
- Multi-value OR: `regexp_matches("col", '(?i)(term1|term2)')`
- No vocabulary available: default to `ILIKE '%term%'`

WITH performance_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
        "[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
        "[Employee Master[Department]]",
        "[Employee Master[Email Address]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_calculated_rating,
        MAX(rec['Employee Performance[Normalized Performance Rating]']) AS best_rating_description
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(perf)
    WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]", "[Employee Master[Position Title]]", "[Employee Master[Department]]", "[Employee Master[Email Address]]"
),
competency_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_supervisor_rating
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Competencies Rating[Competancy Name][Employee Rating][Supervisor Rating][Employee Rating Description][Supervisor Rating Description]]") AS t(comp)
    WHERE rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
)
SELECT 
    p.[Employee Master[Employee Name]],
    p.[Employee Master[Employee Grade]],
    p.[Employee Master[Position Title]],
    p.[Employee Master[Department]],
    p.max_calculated_rating,
    p.best_rating_description,
    COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS max_supervisor_rating,
    COALESCE(p.max_calculated_rating, 0) + COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS combined_score
FROM performance_data p
LEFT JOIN competency_data c ON p."Employee Master[Employee Name]" = c."Employee Master[Employee Name]"
ORDER BY combined_score DESC
LIMIT 5;
"""

### 🔍 DUCKDB SEARCH STRATEGY

Use `execute_sql_query(sql)`. The `"samples"` field is your vocabulary.

⚠️ **CRITICAL SYNTAX RULES — violations cause runtime errors:**
1. Column names in double quotes MUST include outer brackets: `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` NOT `"Employee Master[Employee Name]"`. The `[...]` is part of the name.
2. UNNEST MUST use `AS t(rec)` alias pattern — then access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER use `AS pe` or `pe['field']` — DuckDB requires the `(rec)` destructuring.
3. Copy column/field names from the listings below **character-for-character** — do NOT abbreviate or reformat them.

**THREE COLUMN TYPES — READ CAREFULLY:**
- **Flat columns**: top-level columns, accessed as `"column_name"` directly.
- **Identifier columns**: unique-value columns (IDs, keys), use for JOINs and exact lookups.
- **Struct columns**: nested columns containing multiple fields, accessed via UNNEST.

**FLAT COLUMNS** (use `"col_name"` directly in SELECT/WHERE):
  → `"[CV Employee Summary[CV Employee Summary]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Age]]"` (float)

**Rule 1 — freetext columns**: Use `ILIKE '%word%'`. Find closest word in samples first. For typos: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') > 0.85`.
  → `"[CV Employee Summary[CV Employee Summary]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Educational Institute]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name (Arabic)]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Major (Education)]]"` is freetext

**Rule 2 — categorical columns**: Use `=` or `IN (...)` with exact values from samples.
  → `"[Employee Master[Assignment Status]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Degree]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Department]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Division]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Gender]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Grade]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Job Title]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Reason]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Marital Status]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Nationality]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Children]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Office Name]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Organization Unit]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Person Type]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Position Title]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Sector]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Supervisor Name]]"` is categorical

**Rule 3 — struct columns**: Copy column/field names from the STRUCT block above EXACTLY.
- Flat columns are accessed as `"column_name"` — they are NOT inside any UNNEST.
- Struct fields are accessed as `rec['field_name']` — ONLY after UNNEST.
- NEVER use `t.rec["flat_col"]` for flat columns — use `"flat_col"` directly.
- ⚠️ UNNEST alias MUST be `AS t(rec)` — the `(rec)` is required. Access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER write `AS pe` or `pe['field']`.
```sql
-- ✅ CORRECT: UNNEST with AS t(rec), access fields as rec['field']
SELECT "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS rating
FROM enterprise_data
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL;

-- ❌ WRONG: Missing outer brackets on column name, wrong UNNEST alias
SELECT "Employee Master[Employee Name]", pe['Calculated Rating']  -- BOTH WRONG!
FROM enterprise_data CROSS JOIN UNNEST("...") AS pe;  -- pe['field'] does NOT work!

-- MULTIPLE structs: use SEPARATE aliases t1, t2 (NOT t.something)
SELECT "flat_col", t1.rec['field_a'] AS a, t2.rec['field_b'] AS b
FROM my_table
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_1") AS t1(rec)
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_2") AS t2(rec)
WHERE t1.rec['field_a'] = 'value';

-- Filter rows via list_filter (for boolean checks without UNNEST):
SELECT * FROM my_table
WHERE len(list_filter("struct_col", x -> x['field'] ILIKE '%term%')) > 0;

-- Aggregate inside structs:
SELECT "flat_col", SUM(rec['numeric_field']) AS total
FROM my_table CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col") AS t(rec)
GROUP BY "flat_col";
```
Inside structs, apply Rule 1 (freetext→ILIKE) or Rule 2 (categorical→=) based on each field's semantic_type.

**Rule 4 — search toolkit**:
- Case-insensitive: `ILIKE '%term%'` (default for user text search)
- Accent-insensitive: `lower(strip_accents("col")) ILIKE lower(strip_accents('%term%'))`
- Fuzzy names: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') >= 0.85`
- Multi-value OR: `regexp_matches("col", '(?i)(term1|term2)')`
- No vocabulary available: default to `ILIKE '%term%'`

### QUERY

import pandas as pd
sql_query = """
SELECT 
    "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
    "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
    "[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
    "[Employee Master[Department]]",

    rec['Employee Performance[Performance Review Period]'] AS review_period
FROM enterprise_data
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL

ORDER BY

LIMIT 5;

sql_query_3 = """
WITH performance_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
        "[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
        "[Employee Master[Department]]",
        "[Employee Master[Email Address]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_calculated_rating,
        MAX(rec['Employee Performance[Normalized Performance Rating]']) AS best_rating_description
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(perf)
    WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]", "[Employee Master[Position Title]]", "[Employee Master[Department]]", "[Employee Master[Email Address]]"
),
competency_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_supervisor_rating
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Competencies Rating[Competancy Name][Employee Rating][Supervisor Rating][Employee Rating Description][Supervisor Rating Description]]") AS t(comp)
    WHERE rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
)
SELECT 
    p.[Employee Master[Employee Name]],
    p.[Employee Master[Employee Grade]],
    p.[Employee Master[Position Title]],
    p.[Employee Master[Department]],
    p.max_calculated_rating,
    p.best_rating_description,
    COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS max_supervisor_rating,
    COALESCE(p.max_calculated_rating, 0) + COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS combined_score
FROM performance_data p
LEFT JOIN competency_data c ON p."Employee Master[Employee Name]" = c."Employee Master[Employee Name]"
ORDER BY combined_score DESC
LIMIT 5;
"""
result_df_3 = execute_sql_query(sql_query_3)

At the end of the generated code, you MUST declare a "result" variable as a dictionary of type and value in the following format:

type (possible values "string", "number", "dataframe", "plot"). Examples: { "type": "string", "value": f"The highest salary is {highest_salary}." } or { "type": "number", "value": 125 } or { "type": "dataframe", "value": pd.DataFrame({...}) } or { "type": "plot", "value": "temp_chart.png" }

Do NOT use the `return` keyword, as the code will be executed in the global scope.

The following functions have already been provided. Please use them as needed and do not redefine them.
<function>
def execute_sql_query(sql_query: str) -> pd.DataFrame
    """Execute a DuckDB SQL query and return the result as a pandas DataFrame."""
</function>


### 🔍 DUCKDB SEARCH STRATEGY

Use `execute_sql_query(sql)`. The `"samples"` field is your vocabulary.

⚠️ **CRITICAL SYNTAX RULES — violations cause runtime errors:**
1. Column names in double quotes MUST include outer brackets: `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` NOT `"Employee Master[Employee Name]"`. The `[...]` is part of the name.
2. UNNEST MUST use `AS t(rec)` alias pattern — then access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER use `AS pe` or `pe['field']` — DuckDB requires the `(rec)` destructuring.
3. Copy column/field names from the listings below **character-for-character** — do NOT abbreviate or reformat them.

**THREE COLUMN TYPES — READ CAREFULLY:**
- **Flat columns**: top-level columns, accessed as `"column_name"` directly.
- **Identifier columns**: unique-value columns (IDs, keys), use for JOINs and exact lookups.
- **Struct columns**: nested columns containing multiple fields, accessed via UNNEST.

**FLAT COLUMNS** (use `"col_name"` directly in SELECT/WHERE):
  → `"[CV Employee Summary[CV Employee Summary]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Age]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Assignment Status]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Basic Salary]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Child Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Cost of Living Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Date of Joining]]"` (datetime)
  → `"[Employee Master[Degree]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Department]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Division]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Educational Institute]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name (Arabic)]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Family Book Number]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Gender]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Grade]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Graduation Date]]"` (datetime)
  → `"[Employee Master[Housing Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Job Title]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Date]]"` (datetime)
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Reason]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Major (Education)]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Marital Status]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Nationality]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Children]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Spouses]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Office Name]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Organization Unit]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Person Type]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Phone Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Position Title]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Sector]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Sick Leave Taken]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Social Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Supervisor Name]]"` (string, categorical)
  → `"[Employee Master[Supplementary Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Technical Special Allowance]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Time Since Last Promotion]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Total Entitlement Amount]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Annual Leave Entitlement]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Approved Annual Leave]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Approved Non-Mandatory Leave]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Approved Wellbeing Leave]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Non-Mandatory Leave Entitlement]]"` (float)
  → `"[Entitlement Leaves[Wellbeing Leave Entitlement]]"` (float)

**IDENTIFIER COLUMNS** (use for JOINs and exact lookups only — do NOT filter by arbitrary values):
  → `"[Employee Master[Email Address]]"` (id_like, e.g. akalghfeli@ECOUNCIL.AE, eraldhaheri@ECOUNCIL.AE, nmsalmenhali@ECOUNCIL.AE)
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Number]]"` (id_like, e.g. 1269, 1314, 1316)
  → `"[Employee Master[Passport Number]]"` (id_like, e.g. AA0095550, AA046978, AA0623273)

**STRUCT COLUMNS** (require UNNEST — see Rule 3):

**Rule 1 — freetext columns**: Use `ILIKE '%word%'`. Find closest word in samples first. For typos: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') > 0.85`.
  → `"[CV Employee Summary[CV Employee Summary]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Educational Institute]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name (Arabic)]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Major (Education)]]"` is freetext

**Rule 2 — categorical columns**: Use `=` or `IN (...)` with exact values from samples.
  → `"[Employee Master[Assignment Status]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Degree]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Department]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Division]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Gender]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Grade]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Job Title]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Reason]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Marital Status]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Nationality]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Children]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Office Name]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Organization Unit]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Person Type]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Position Title]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Sector]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Supervisor Name]]"` is categorical

**Rule 3 — struct columns**: Copy column/field names from the STRUCT block above EXACTLY.
- Flat columns are accessed as `"column_name"` — they are NOT inside any UNNEST.
- Struct fields are accessed as `rec['field_name']` — ONLY after UNNEST.
- NEVER use `t.rec["flat_col"]` for flat columns — use `"flat_col"` directly.
- ⚠️ UNNEST alias MUST be `AS t(rec)` — the `(rec)` is required. Access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER write `AS pe` or `pe['field']`.
```sql
-- ✅ CORRECT: UNNEST with AS t(rec), access fields as rec['field']
SELECT "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS rating
FROM enterprise_data
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL;

-- ❌ WRONG: Missing outer brackets on column name, wrong UNNEST alias
SELECT "Employee Master[Employee Name]", pe['Calculated Rating']  -- BOTH WRONG!
FROM enterprise_data CROSS JOIN UNNEST("...") AS pe;  -- pe['field'] does NOT work!

-- MULTIPLE structs: use SEPARATE aliases t1, t2 (NOT t.something)
SELECT "flat_col", t1.rec['field_a'] AS a, t2.rec['field_b'] AS b
FROM my_table
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_1") AS t1(rec)
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_2") AS t2(rec)
WHERE t1.rec['field_a'] = 'value';

-- Filter rows via list_filter (for boolean checks without UNNEST):
SELECT * FROM my_table
WHERE len(list_filter("struct_col", x -> x['field'] ILIKE '%term%')) > 0;

-- Aggregate inside structs:
SELECT "flat_col", SUM(rec['numeric_field']) AS total
FROM my_table CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col") AS t(rec)
GROUP BY "flat_col";
```
Inside structs, apply Rule 1 (freetext→ILIKE) or Rule 2 (categorical→=) based on each field's semantic_type.

**Rule 4 — search toolkit**:
- Case-insensitive: `ILIKE '%term%'` (default for user text search)
- Accent-insensitive: `lower(strip_accents("col")) ILIKE lower(strip_accents('%term%'))`
- Fuzzy names: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') >= 0.85`
- Multi-value OR: `regexp_matches("col", '(?i)(term1|term2)')`
- No vocabulary available: default to `ILIKE '%term%'`

 who is the best employee?

You generated the following Python code:
import pandas as pd
sql_query_3 = """
WITH performance_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
        "[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
        "[Employee Master[Department]]",
        "[Employee Master[Email Address]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_calculated_rating,
        MAX(rec['Employee Performance[Normalized Performance Rating]']) AS best_rating_description
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
    WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]", "[Employee Master[Position Title]]", "[Employee Master[Department]]", "[Employee Master[Email Address]]"
),
competency_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_supervisor_rating
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Competencies Rating[Competancy Name][Employee Rating][Supervisor Rating][Employee Rating Description][Supervisor Rating Description]]") AS t(rec)
    WHERE rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
)
SELECT 
    p."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Department]]",
    p.max_calculated_rating,
    p.best_rating_description,
    COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS max_supervisor_rating,
    COALESCE(p.max_calculated_rating, 0) + COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS combined_score
FROM performance_data p
LEFT JOIN competency_data c ON p."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]" = c."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
ORDER BY combined_score DESC
LIMIT 5;
"""
result_df_3 = execute_sql_query(sql_query_3)

print('Analysis Complete. Showing top performers based on calculated performance ratings and supervisor ratings.')
print(result)

### 🔍 DUCKDB SEARCH STRATEGY

Use `execute_sql_query(sql)`. The `"samples"` field is your vocabulary.

⚠️ **CRITICAL SYNTAX RULES — violations cause runtime errors:**
1. Column names in double quotes MUST include outer brackets: `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` NOT `"Employee Master[Employee Name]"`. The `[...]` is part of the name.
2. UNNEST MUST use `AS t(rec)` alias pattern — then access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER use `AS pe` or `pe['field']` — DuckDB requires the `(rec)` destructuring.
3. Copy column/field names from the listings below **character-for-character** — do NOT abbreviate or reformat them.

**THREE COLUMN TYPES — READ CAREFULLY:**
- **Flat columns**: top-level columns, accessed as `"column_name"` directly.
- **Identifier columns**: unique-value columns (IDs, keys), use for JOINs and exact lookups.
- **Struct columns**: nested columns containing multiple fields, accessed via UNNEST.

**FLAT COLUMNS** (use `"col_name"` directly in SELECT/WHERE):
  → `"[CV Employee Summary[CV Employee Summary]]"` (string, freetext)
  → `"[Employee Master[Age]]"` (float)
  → `"[Employee Master[Assignment Status]]"` (string, categorical)

**Rule 1 — freetext columns**: Use `ILIKE '%word%'`. Find closest word in samples first. For typos: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') > 0.85`.
  → `"[CV Employee Summary[CV Employee Summary]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Educational Institute]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Name (Arabic)]]"` is freetext
  → `"[Employee Master[Major (Education)]]"` is freetext

**Rule 2 — categorical columns**: Use `=` or `IN (...)` with exact values from samples.
  → `"[Employee Master[Assignment Status]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Degree]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Department]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Division]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Gender]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Grade]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Job Title]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Last Promotion Reason]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Marital Status]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Nationality]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Number of Children]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Office Name]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Organization Unit]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Person Type]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Position Title]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Sector]]"` is categorical
  → `"[Employee Master[Supervisor Name]]"` is categorical

**Rule 3 — struct columns**: Copy column/field names from the STRUCT block above EXACTLY.
- Flat columns are accessed as `"column_name"` — they are NOT inside any UNNEST.
- Struct fields are accessed as `rec['field_name']` — ONLY after UNNEST.
- NEVER use `t.rec["flat_col"]` for flat columns — use `"flat_col"` directly.
- ⚠️ UNNEST alias MUST be `AS t(rec)` — the `(rec)` is required. Access fields as `rec['field']`. NEVER write `AS pe` or `pe['field']`.
```sql
-- ✅ CORRECT: UNNEST with AS t(rec), access fields as rec['field']
SELECT "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS rating
FROM enterprise_data
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL;

-- ❌ WRONG: Missing outer brackets on column name, wrong UNNEST alias
SELECT "Employee Master[Employee Name]", pe['Calculated Rating']  -- BOTH WRONG!
FROM enterprise_data CROSS JOIN UNNEST("...") AS pe;  -- pe['field'] does NOT work!

-- MULTIPLE structs: use SEPARATE aliases t1, t2 (NOT t.something)
SELECT "flat_col", t1.rec['field_a'] AS a, t2.rec['field_b'] AS b
FROM my_table
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_1") AS t1(rec)
CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col_2") AS t2(rec)
WHERE t1.rec['field_a'] = 'value';

-- Filter rows via list_filter (for boolean checks without UNNEST):
SELECT * FROM my_table
WHERE len(list_filter("struct_col", x -> x['field'] ILIKE '%term%')) > 0;

-- Aggregate inside structs:
SELECT "flat_col", SUM(rec['numeric_field']) AS total
FROM my_table CROSS JOIN UNNEST("struct_col") AS t(rec)
GROUP BY "flat_col";
```
Inside structs, apply Rule 1 (freetext→ILIKE) or Rule 2 (categorical→=) based on each field's semantic_type.

**Rule 4 — search toolkit**:
- Case-insensitive: `ILIKE '%term%'` (default for user text search)
- Accent-insensitive: `lower(strip_accents("col")) ILIKE lower(strip_accents('%term%'))`
- Fuzzy names: `jaro_winkler_similarity(LOWER("col"), 'term') >= 0.85`
- Multi-value OR: `regexp_matches("col", '(?i)(term1|term2)')`
- No vocabulary available: default to `ILIKE '%term%'`

### QUERY
 who is the best employee?

You generated the following Python code:
import pandas as pd
sql_query_3 = """
WITH performance_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
        "[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
        "[Employee Master[Department]]",
        "[Employee Master[Email Address]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_calculated_rating,
        MAX(rec['Employee Performance[Normalized Performance Rating]']) AS best_rating_description
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
    WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]", "[Employee Master[Position Title]]", "[Employee Master[Department]]", "[Employee Master[Email Address]]"
),
competency_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_supervisor_rating
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Competencies Rating[Competancy Name][Employee Rating][Supervisor Rating][Employee Rating Description][Supervisor Rating Description]]") AS t(rec)
    WHERE rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
)
SELECT 
    p."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Department]]",
    p.max_calculated_rating,
    p.best_rating_description,
    COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS max_supervisor_rating,
    COALESCE(p.max_calculated_rating, 0) + COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS combined_score
FROM performance_data p
LEFT JOIN competency_data c ON p."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]" = c."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
ORDER BY combined_score DESC
LIMIT 5;
"""
result_df_3 = execute_sql_query(sql_query_3)
result = {'top_performer': result_df_3.iloc[0]['[Employee Master[Employee Name]]'] if len(result_df_3) > 0 else 'No data available', 'top_performer_details': result_df_3.iloc[0].to_dict() if len(result_df_3) > 0 else {}, 'top_performers_5': result_df_3.to_dict('records') if len(result_df_3) > 0 else []}
print('Analysis Complete. Showing top performers based on calculated performance ratings and supervisor ratings.')
print(result)

    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_supervisor_rating
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Competencies Rating[Competancy Name][Employee Rating][Supervisor Rating][Employee Rating Description][Supervisor Rating Description]]") AS t(rec)
    WHERE rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
)
SELECT 
    p."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Department]]",
    p.max_calculated_rating,
    p.best_rating_description,
    COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS max_supervisor_rating,
    COALESCE(p.max_calculated_rating, 0) + COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS combined_score
FROM performance_data p
LEFT JOIN competency_data c ON p."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]" = c."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
ORDER BY combined_score DESC
LIMIT 5;
"""

result_df_3 = execute_sql_query(sql_query_3)

# Determine the top performer for summary
top_performer = result_df_3.iloc[0]['[Employee Master[Employee Name]]'] if len(result_df_3) > 0 else 'No data available'

# Create the required result format
result = {
    "type": "dataframe",
    "value": result_df_3
}

print('Analysis Complete. Showing top performers based on calculated performance ratings and supervisor ratings.')
print(f"Top performer: {top_performer}")
print(result)

sql_query_3 = """
WITH performance_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
        "[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
        "[Employee Master[Department]]",
        "[Employee Master[Email Address]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_calculated_rating,
        MAX(rec['Employee Performance[Normalized Performance Rating]']) AS best_rating_description
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Performance[Performance Review Period][Calculated Rating][Normalized Performance Rating]]") AS t(rec)
    WHERE rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Performance[Calculated Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]", "[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]", "[Employee Master[Position Title]]", "[Employee Master[Department]]", "[Employee Master[Email Address]]"
),
competency_data AS (
    SELECT 
        "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
        MAX(CAST(rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] AS FLOAT)) AS max_supervisor_rating
    FROM enterprise_data
    CROSS JOIN UNNEST("[Employee Competencies Rating[Competancy Name][Employee Rating][Supervisor Rating][Employee Rating Description][Supervisor Rating Description]]") AS t(rec)
    WHERE rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] IS NOT NULL 
        AND rec['Employee Competencies Rating[Supervisor Rating]'] != ''
    GROUP BY "[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
)
SELECT 
    p."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Employee Grade]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Position Title]]",
    p."[Employee Master[Department]]",
    p.max_calculated_rating,
    p.best_rating_description,
    COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS max_supervisor_rating,
    COALESCE(p.max_calculated_rating, 0) + COALESCE(c.max_supervisor_rating, 0) AS combined_score
FROM performance_data p
LEFT JOIN competency_data c ON p."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]" = c."[Employee Master[Employee Name]]"
ORDER BY combined_score DESC
LIMIT 5;
"""
result_df_3 = execute_sql_query(sql_query_3)
top_performer = result_df_3.iloc[0]['[Employee Master[Employee Name]]'] if len(result_df_3) > 0 else 'No data available'
result = {'type': 'dataframe', 'value': result_df_3}
print('Analysis Complete. Showing top performers based on calculated performance ratings and supervisor ratings.')
print(f'Top performer: {top_performer}')
print(result)
